You are analyzing **one block of a longer dialogue** to extract actions.

**ACTION** = An atomic communicative act a speaker **directly performed** in a turn.
- Atomic action = minimal unit of action the speaker directly performed. - e.g.
"User asks to find a paper about HCI in NLP conferences" → split into "User asks
to find a paper about HCI", "User asks to find a paper in NLP conferences".
- Include evidence quote from the utterance.

**Possible action types** (generate new types if needed):
Accept, Acknowledge, Address, Allow, Analyze, Apologize, Ask, Challenge, Clarify,
Classify, Compare, Complain, Confirm, Connect, Constrain, Critique, Decide, Define,
Delegate, Describe, Draft, Emphasize, Evaluate, Explain, Feedback, Formalize, Frame,
Greet, Hypothesize, Implement, Include, Infer, Instruct, Invite, Justify, List,
Modify, Observe, Plan, Provide, Qualify, Recommend, Refuse, Report, Request, State,
Suggest, Summarize, Warn

**Role:** **SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER**
* **SHAPER:** Creates/shapes/revises the goal/requirement by proposing new ideas,
new tasks, constraints, alternatives, or directions.
* **EXECUTOR:** Executes/achieves the desired outcome/goal/requirement (e.g.,
drafting text, providing requested information, coding, searching, implementing).
* **OTHER:** Neither SHAPER nor EXECUTOR.

**Turn ID format:** each turn is labeled **U(x,y)** or **A(x,y)**, where **U**=user
turn, **A**=AI turn, **x**=the pair number this turn belongs to (the 1st user+AI
exchange is pair 1, the 2nd is pair 2, etc.), and **y**=the action's position
within that turn (1, 2, 3… in the order it was extracted). e.g. the user's 2nd
atomic action in pair 1 is "U(1,2)"; the AI's 1st atomic action in pair 3 is
"A(3,1)". Use this format for every turn id below — do not use plain integers.

So they've got a really compelling concept here—treating Jayon as a subject in a nature documentary, with Attenborough's distinctive observational narration style applied to a creative technologist. They're planning to blend AI-generated visuals with that voiceover, possibly anchoring it with real footage of him at the end. They've already developed the script and dialogue, and they're working from existing writings they've prepared.

I should wait for them to share their material rather than jump ahead with suggestions. The Attenborough framing is genuinely clever—it sidesteps self-promotion through humor and third-person distance, subtly positions him as something rare and worth observing, and gives the whole thing a natural narrative arc that maps onto a career story. It's also immediately recognizable and memorable to the people who'd be watching this.

One thing I should flag lightly though: we can't actually use David Attenborough's voice or likeness, but we can absolutely do an Attenborough-style narration—that plummy British natural-history tone that evokes the genre without impersonating the man. I'll mention it as a quick note for when we get to the voice work, since it's a real constraint that shapes the script direction, but I won't belabor it since they asked me to wait.

For now I'll just affirm the concept is strong, note that one constraint about the voice style, and hold for their script and writings—no more questions, no jumping ahead.

Done

Locked. The spine is a third-person nature-documentary narration — Attenborough-style, observing "the creative technologist" as if he were a rare species in his habitat. AI hybrid filmmaking carries the visuals, the narration carries the story, and you close on real footage of yourself.

That's a genuinely sharp concept, and worth naming why it works so we protect it:

It solves the self-promo cringe problem. You're not bragging about yourself — a wry, affectionate narrator is observing you. Third person + nature-doc warmth lets you say big things about yourself without it landing as ego.

The genre hands you a free structure. Nature docs have a built-in arc — habitat, behaviour, adaptation, the rare moment — which maps cleanly onto an origin-to-now career journey. Your script probably already feels this even if you didn't name it.

It's memorable and instantly legible. A recruiter watching 30 portfolios remembers "the guy who did the Attenborough thing about himself."

It quietly proves the AI-film skill while being about you — which is exactly the redundancy line we drew. The medium is doing double duty.

One thing to park for the voice stage (not now, just so it doesn't bite us later): we'll do Attenborough-style — a plummy British natural-history VO that evokes the genre — not a clone of his actual voice. Cloning a real person's voice is a likeness problem we don't want on a public portfolio. The style is yours to use freely; the man's voice isn't. Noted, parked.

I'll hold here. Send the script and writings whenever you're ready and we'll build from your material, not from scratch.

Step 1b: Outcome Extraction

You are given ALL actions extracted from a complete dialogue. Your task:
(1) Identify **outcomes** (purpose-linked desired deliverables)
(2) Assign every action to exactly one outcome
(3) Maintain the dialogue summary: if the collaboration's core purpose is
unchanged since the prior summary, return it unchanged; if new actions
materially extend or shift that purpose, revise the summary to reflect the
fuller picture. Keep it to 1-2 sentences regardless of which case applies.

Every collaboration has a **purpose**. **Outcomes are purpose-linked desired
deliverables** --- concrete outputs that participants are actually working toward.
- **Outcomes = purpose-linked desired deliverables:** Deliverables are not limited
to text or documents --- they can be a **decision**, **advice**, **plan**,
**clarification**, or any other concrete result. Do **not** include things only
mentioned without being adopted as a goal. - **Requested or agreed outputs:** Any
concrete output a participant **explicitly requests** or participants **agree to
produce** is a deliverable. - **Primary output:** The main thing the dialogue aims
to produce must appear as an outcome. Sub-deliverables are children. - **Phrasing:**
e.g. "decision for X", "advice for X", "draft for X", "plan for X". - **Hierarchy:**
**parent** = abstract/general, **child** = specific/concrete. Use parent outcome id
/ child outcome ids. - **Granularity:** Prefer consolidated, task-level outcomes.
Example: "Workshop plan (date, venue, agenda)" NOT separate "Decide date", "Decide
time", etc. - **No duplicates.** Each distinct deliverable exactly once.

=== PRIOR REQUIREMENTS FOR THIS OUTCOME (if any; empty on first extraction) ===

=== PRIOR OPEN SLOTS FOR THIS OUTCOME (if any; empty on first extraction) ===
[]

**REQUIREMENT** = An atomic, externally verifiable SUCCESS CONDITION for an outcome.
- Binary testable (pass/fail). - Must be explicitly stated or adopted as binding in
the dialogue.

**NOT a requirement:** content itself, advice, implementation methods, internal
reasoning, example outputs, one-off decisions.

Extract ONLY if ALL pass:
1) NECESSITY --- framed as mandatory (must/need/required/cannot, numeric
constraints, explicit include/exclude)
2) GROUNDING --- directly stated, no inference
3) REPLACEABILITY --- cannot be swapped without violating success
4) BINARY TESTABILITY --- reviewer can judge pass/fail

If an action raises a substantive idea, constraint, or decision point but FAILS
gate (1) NECESSITY specifically because of hedge or uncertainty language — this
includes option-uncertainty ("maybe", "could", "might", "may", "would possibly",
"perhaps", "mix / pick later"), epistemic hedges ("I think", "it seems", "I
believe", "it appears" — used to qualify the CONTENT of the claim, not as a
conversational filler elsewhere in the sentence), or approximation language
("about", "some", "most", "somewhat", "probably", "likely") — OR because the
action commits to determining something later rather than specifying it now
("I'll recommend based on your answer", "I'll decide after", "I'll verify before
committing") — do NOT discard it. Use the open_slot operation (see below) instead
of create. This applies only to Necessity failures caused by hedging, uncertainty,
or deferred content; failures of gates 2-4, content that is not substantive at all
(small talk, pure acknowledgment, restating something already said), or content
where a hedge word appears only as filler alongside an otherwise clearly mandatory
statement (e.g. "I think this must include X" — still a requirement, the hedge
qualifies the speaker's tone, not the requirement's content), are still handled
normally (dropped or extracted as create, respectively).

Operations:
- **create**: New requirement. Check PRIOR REQUIREMENTS above for revise BEFORE
create. - **revise**: Modify existing requirement (contradicts/tightens/
relaxes/replaces). Use EXACT existing req id (from PRIOR REQUIREMENTS) in related
to (within this outcome). - **delete**: Explicitly cancels a previously binding
condition. - **open_slot**: A hedged, uncertain, or deferred-content idea that
fails Necessity but is substantive enough to track — neither a binding requirement
nor nothing. Check PRIOR OPEN SLOTS above for resolve BEFORE open_slot: if this
action firmly answers/resolves a prior open slot, emit op="resolve" instead,
turning that slot into a new create with related to pointing at the resolved slot
id. Assign origin: "user" if the hedge came from the user's action, "AI" if from
the AI's action. A slot that has appeared in PRIOR OPEN SLOTS for 3 or more
consecutive pairs without being resolved should be marked op="abandon" — it is no
longer carried forward.

Edge cases:
- Advice itself is NOT a requirement. Only constraints ON the advice. - "Try to /
ideally / could / maybe" → not binding → not a requirement → check if substantive
enough for open_slot (see above); if not even substantive, drop entirely with no op.

======================== TASK ========================
For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

UNDERSPECIFIED INTENT / PREFERENCE
Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement
Context-Grounded-Requirement | prior context, code, data, or situational
information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements

**contribution role**: SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER. Each listed action already
shows a role. If your judgment disagrees with the role shown, note it in
explanation rather than silently changing it.

Default to NO CONNECTION unless clear semantic evidence.

======================== TASK ========================
For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

UNDERSPECIFIED INTENT / PREFERENCE
Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement
Context-Grounded-Requirement | prior context, code, data, or situational
information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements

**contribution role**: SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER. Each listed action already
shows a role. If your judgment disagrees with the role shown, note it in
explanation rather than silently changing it.

Default to NO CONNECTION unless clear semantic evidence.

======================== TASK ========================
For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

UNDERSPECIFIED INTENT / PREFERENCE
Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement
Context-Grounded-Requirement | prior context, code, data, or situational
information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements

**contribution role**: SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER. Each listed action already
shows a role. If your judgment disagrees with the role shown, note it in
explanation rather than silently changing it.

Default to NO CONNECTION unless clear semantic evidence.

new tasks, constraints, alternatives, or directions.
* **EXECUTOR:** Executes/achieves the desired outcome/goal/requirement (e.g.,
drafting text, providing requested information, coding, searching, implementing).

The user has shared a rough draft script written in Attenborough's nature-documentary style, treating Jayon as a specimen under observation, complete with typos and a rushed ending they're aware of. The narrative arc traces a clear path: the specimen's reveal in the lab, his origins in Kerala, his self-identification as a creative technologist, his early immersion in computer graphics and simulated realities, his gradual shift from algorithms toward filmmaking and media work, his stint as a software engineer at EY, his decision to leave that stability to pursue his passion, and his current life as a Digital Media Master's student in Bremen. The script is showing him evolving into new behaviors and directions, though the ending needs more energy and specificity to land properly.

The nature-documentary vocabulary is already working well — specimen, habitat, migration, behavior, evolution — and the migration metaphor is particularly strong since his actual journey from Kerala to Germany mirrors the migration sequences that documentaries love. The real weakness is that final beat: "excited to take on challenges with creativity and technology on his side" feels generic and deflates the momentum. A strong nature-doc ending either leaves you with a poignant reflection, a sense of what's to come for this creature, or a quiet, memorable image. Right now it's just... enthusiasm. This draft is better than you think. Two things in it are genuinely strong and we should protect them:

The specimen-in-a-cylinder cold open ties straight into your whole site's brain-as-black-box theme — the creature being examined is the black box. And "migration" is a gift: Kerala → Germany is literally a migration, and nature docs live for migration sequences. You stumbled onto the perfect metaphor.

Here's the structural thing you half-did without naming it, and it's the key to the whole script: the narrator says "it," never "he." Calling a human "the specimen / the subject / it" is the entire joke and the entire warmth. So the spine is a pronoun shift — the film observes an object ("it") for 50 seconds, then hard-cuts to the real you, where it becomes "I." Observed thing → speaking person. That shift is your ending. That's why the current ending feels lazy — it's not badly written, it just trails off in third person instead of landing on the turn.

Now the hard constraint, because it explains the sluggish back half: Attenborough narration is slow — maybe 110–130 words per minute with the dramatic pauses. The fix is to cut the middle, not pad the ending.

Step 1b: Outcome Extraction

You are given ALL actions extracted from a complete dialogue. Your task:
(1) Identify **outcomes** (purpose-linked desired deliverables)
(2) Assign every action to exactly one outcome
(3) Maintain the dialogue summary: if the collaboration's core purpose is
unchanged since the prior summary, return it unchanged; if new actions
materially extend or shift that purpose, revise the summary to reflect the
fuller picture. Keep it to 1-2 sentences regardless of which case applies.

Every collaboration has a **purpose**. **Outcomes are purpose-linked desired
deliverables** --- concrete outputs that participants are actually working toward.
- **Outcomes = purpose-linked desired deliverables:** Deliverables are not limited
to text or documents --- they can be a **decision**, **advice**, **plan**,
**clarification**, or any other concrete result. Do **not** include things only
mentioned without being adopted as a goal. - **Requested or agreed outputs:** Any
concrete output a participant **explicitly requests** or participants **agree to
produce** is a deliverable. - **Primary output:** The main thing the dialogue aims
to produce must appear as an outcome. Sub-deliverables are children. - **Phrasing:**
e.g. "decision for X", "advice for X", "draft for X", "plan for X". - **Hierarchy:**
**parent** = abstract/general, **child** = specific/concrete. Use parent outcome id
/ child outcome ids. - **Granularity:** Prefer consolidated, task-level outcomes.
Example: "Workshop plan (date, venue, agenda)" NOT separate "Decide date", "Decide
time", etc. - **No duplicates.** Each distinct deliverable exactly once.

**REQUIREMENT** = An atomic, externally verifiable SUCCESS CONDITION for an outcome.
- Binary testable (pass/fail). - Must be explicitly stated or adopted as binding in
the dialogue.

**NOT a requirement:** content itself, advice, implementation methods, internal
reasoning, example outputs, one-off decisions.

Extract ONLY if ALL pass:
1) NECESSITY --- framed as mandatory (must/need/required/cannot, numeric
constraints, explicit include/exclude)
2) GROUNDING --- directly stated, no inference
3) REPLACEABILITY --- cannot be swapped without violating success
4) BINARY TESTABILITY --- reviewer can judge pass/fail

If an action raises a substantive idea, constraint, or decision point but FAILS
gate (1) NECESSITY specifically because of hedge or uncertainty language — this
includes option-uncertainty ("maybe", "could", "might", "may", "would possibly",
"perhaps", "mix / pick later"), epistemic hedges ("I think", "it seems", "I
believe", "it appears" — used to qualify the CONTENT of the claim, not as a
conversational filler elsewhere in the sentence), or approximation language
("about", "some", "most", "somewhat", "probably", "likely") — OR because the
action commits to determining something later rather than specifying it now
("I'll recommend based on your answer", "I'll decide after", "I'll verify before
committing") — do NOT discard it. Use the open_slot operation (see below) instead
of create. This applies only to Necessity failures caused by hedging, uncertainty,
or deferred content; failures of gates 2-4, content that is not substantive at all
(small talk, pure acknowledgment, restating something already said), or content
where a hedge word appears only as filler alongside an otherwise clearly mandatory
statement (e.g. "I think this must include X" — still a requirement, the hedge
qualifies the speaker's tone, not the requirement's content), are still handled
normally (dropped or extracted as create, respectively).

Operations:
- **create**: New requirement. Check PRIOR REQUIREMENTS above for revise BEFORE
create. - **revise**: Modify existing requirement (contradicts/tightens/
relaxes/replaces). Use EXACT existing req id (from PRIOR REQUIREMENTS) in related
to (within this outcome). - **delete**: Explicitly cancels a previously binding
condition. - **open_slot**: A hedged, uncertain, or deferred-content idea that
fails Necessity but is substantive enough to track — neither a binding requirement
nor nothing. Check PRIOR OPEN SLOTS above for resolve BEFORE open_slot: if this
action firmly answers/resolves a prior open slot, emit op="resolve" instead,
turning that slot into a new create with related to pointing at the resolved slot
id. Assign origin: "user" if the hedge came from the user's action, "AI" if from
the AI's action. A slot that has appeared in PRIOR OPEN SLOTS for 3 or more
consecutive pairs without being resolved should be marked op="abandon" — it is no
longer carried forward.

Edge cases:
- Advice itself is NOT a requirement. Only constraints ON the advice. - "Try to /
ideally / could / maybe" → not binding → not a requirement → check if substantive
enough for open_slot (see above); if not even substantive, drop entirely with no op.

======================== TASK ========================
For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

UNDERSPECIFIED INTENT / PREFERENCE
Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement
Context-Grounded-Requirement | prior context, code, data, or situational
information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements

**contribution role**: SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER. Each listed action already
shows a role. If your judgment disagrees with the role shown, note it in
explanation rather than silently changing it.

Default to NO CONNECTION unless clear semantic evidence.

tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The

directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be

  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still

  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

You are analyzing **one block of a longer dialogue** to extract actions.

**ACTION** = An atomic communicative act a speaker **directly performed** in a turn.
- Atomic action = minimal unit of action the speaker directly performed. - e.g.
"User asks to find a paper about HCI in NLP conferences" → split into "User asks
to find a paper about HCI", "User asks to find a paper in NLP conferences".
- Include evidence quote from the utterance.

**Possible action types** (generate new types if needed):
Accept, Acknowledge, Address, Allow, Analyze, Apologize, Ask, Challenge, Clarify,
Classify, Compare, Complain, Confirm, Connect, Constrain, Critique, Decide, Define,
Delegate, Describe, Draft, Emphasize, Evaluate, Explain, Feedback, Formalize, Frame,
Greet, Hypothesize, Implement, Include, Infer, Instruct, Invite, Justify, List,
Modify, Observe, Plan, Provide, Qualify, Recommend, Refuse, Report, Request, State,
Suggest, Summarize, Warn

**Role:** **SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER**
* **SHAPER:** Creates/shapes/revises the goal/requirement by proposing new ideas,
new tasks, constraints, alternatives, or directions.
* **EXECUTOR:** Executes/achieves the desired outcome/goal/requirement (e.g.,
drafting text, providing requested information, coding, searching, implementing).
* **OTHER:** Neither SHAPER nor EXECUTOR.

**Turn ID format:** each turn is labeled **U(x,y)** or **A(x,y)**, where **U**=user
turn, **A**=AI turn, **x**=the pair number this turn belongs to (the 1st user+AI
exchange is pair 1, the 2nd is pair 2, etc.), and **y**=the action's position
within that turn (1, 2, 3… in the order it was extracted). e.g. the user's 2nd
atomic action in pair 1 is "U(1,2)"; the AI's 1st atomic action in pair 3 is
"A(3,1)". Use this format for every turn id below — do not use plain integers.

Done

Here's what to watch and what to actually listen for — not just "enjoy it," but study it like a writer.

The essential sequences (search these on YouTube):

"Planet Earth II iguana vs snakes" — the single most important one for you. Four and a half minutes, and Attenborough speaks just 89 words total in it. Study how little he says. The visuals carry the tension; he only steps in to frame and release it. This is your word-budget lesson made concrete — your 60-second film should feel this restrained.

"Attenborough bird of paradise courtship" (Life of Birds / Planet Earth II) — the closest match to your tone. It's gently comic, affectionate, slightly bemused at the absurdity of the creature's behaviour. That wry warmth — observing a ridiculous mating dance with total deadpan respect — is exactly the register your "specimen who calls itself a creative technologist" needs.

"Attenborough pebble toad" (Life in Cold Blood) — a tiny creature doing something unexpected, narrated like a heist. Great for studying the build-reveal rhythm: setup → pause → payoff.

"Life of Mammals chimpanzee" or any single-creature profile — watch how he introduces one animal and tracks it through a journey. That's structurally your film: one specimen, observed across its life stages.

What to listen for specifically — these are the writing rules under the voice:

Scripts are written last. He writes only after the footage is shot and the scenes are cut — he watches the edit, then speaks to it. For you that means: don't perfect the script yet. Write loosely, generate visuals, then tighten the words to the picture. Your draft is doing the right thing being rough.

Answer first, then detail. He tells you the main thing up front so you're not holding suspense — you can just absorb the detail. Your "This is Jayon K Vinod" cold open already does this. Keep it.

The pauses are the writing. Long pauses, tight metaphors, never hysterical. Half the effect is silence. Write fewer words and trust the gaps.

Present tense, observational verbs. "It was observed…", "the subject is seen…", "further investigation revealed…" — you already caught this instinct in your draft. That clinical-but-fond framing is the whole device.

One metaphor, used well. He doesn't pile on imagery. Pick your strongest (the migration) and let it breathe.

One practical move: while you watch, pause after each narrated line and transcribe 5–6 of his actual sentences into a notes file. Don't paraphrase — copy them exactly. Then look at the shape: how short, where the verb sits, where the pause falls. You'll internalise the rhythm faster by hand-copying six real sentences than by watching an hour.

When you've watched a couple and have the rhythm in your ear, bring your draft back and we'll rewrite it line by line in that cadence. Want me to wait for that, or rework the current draft now using these rules so you have a target to compare against?

Step 1b: Outcome Extraction

You are given ALL actions extracted from a complete dialogue. Your task:
(1) Identify **outcomes** (purpose-linked desired deliverables)
(2) Assign every action to exactly one outcome
(3) Maintain the dialogue summary: if the collaboration's core purpose is
unchanged since the prior summary, return it unchanged; if new actions
materially extend or shift that purpose, revise the summary to reflect the
fuller picture. Keep it to 1-2 sentences regardless of which case applies.

Every collaboration has a **purpose**. **Outcomes are purpose-linked desired
deliverables** --- concrete outputs that participants are actually working toward.
- **Outcomes = purpose-linked desired deliverables:** Deliverables are not limited
to text or documents --- they can be a **decision**, **advice**, **plan**,
**clarification**, or any other concrete result. Do **not** include things only
mentioned without being adopted as a goal. - **Requested or agreed outputs:** Any
concrete output a participant **explicitly requests** or participants **agree to
produce** is a deliverable. - **Primary output:** The main thing the dialogue aims
to produce must appear as an outcome. Sub-deliverables are children. - **Phrasing:**
e.g. "decision for X", "advice for X", "draft for X", "plan for X". - **Hierarchy:**
**parent** = abstract/general, **child** = specific/concrete. Use parent outcome id
/ child outcome ids. - **Granularity:** Prefer consolidated, task-level outcomes.
Example: "Workshop plan (date, venue, agenda)" NOT separate "Decide date", "Decide
time", etc. - **No duplicates.** Each distinct deliverable exactly once.

=== PRIOR OPEN SLOTS FOR THIS OUTCOME (if any; empty on first extraction) ===

**REQUIREMENT** = An atomic, externally verifiable SUCCESS CONDITION for an outcome.
- Binary testable (pass/fail). - Must be explicitly stated or adopted as binding in
the dialogue.

**NOT a requirement:** content itself, advice, implementation methods, internal
reasoning, example outputs, one-off decisions.

Extract ONLY if ALL pass:
1) NECESSITY --- framed as mandatory (must/need/required/cannot, numeric
constraints, explicit include/exclude)
2) GROUNDING --- directly stated, no inference
3) REPLACEABILITY --- cannot be swapped without violating success
4) BINARY TESTABILITY --- reviewer can judge pass/fail

If an action raises a substantive idea, constraint, or decision point but FAILS
gate (1) NECESSITY specifically because of hedge or uncertainty language — this
includes option-uncertainty ("maybe", "could", "might", "may", "would possibly",
"perhaps", "mix / pick later"), epistemic hedges ("I think", "it seems", "I
believe", "it appears" — used to qualify the CONTENT of the claim, not as a
conversational filler elsewhere in the sentence), or approximation language
("about", "some", "most", "somewhat", "probably", "likely") — OR because the
action commits to determining something later rather than specifying it now
("I'll recommend based on your answer", "I'll decide after", "I'll verify before
committing") — do NOT discard it. Use the open_slot operation (see below) instead
of create. This applies only to Necessity failures caused by hedging, uncertainty,
or deferred content; failures of gates 2-4, content that is not substantive at all
(small talk, pure acknowledgment, restating something already said), or content
where a hedge word appears only as filler alongside an otherwise clearly mandatory
statement (e.g. "I think this must include X" — still a requirement, the hedge
qualifies the speaker's tone, not the requirement's content), are still handled
normally (dropped or extracted as create, respectively).

Operations:
- **create**: New requirement. Check PRIOR REQUIREMENTS above for revise BEFORE
create. - **revise**: Modify existing requirement (contradicts/tightens/
relaxes/replaces). Use EXACT existing req id (from PRIOR REQUIREMENTS) in related
to (within this outcome). - **delete**: Explicitly cancels a previously binding
condition. - **open_slot**: A hedged, uncertain, or deferred-content idea that
fails Necessity but is substantive enough to track — neither a binding requirement
nor nothing. Check PRIOR OPEN SLOTS above for resolve BEFORE open_slot: if this
action firmly answers/resolves a prior open slot, emit op="resolve" instead,
turning that slot into a new create with related to pointing at the resolved slot
id. Assign origin: "user" if the hedge came from the user's action, "AI" if from
the AI's action. A slot that has appeared in PRIOR OPEN SLOTS for 3 or more
consecutive pairs without being resolved should be marked op="abandon" — it is no
longer carried forward.

Edge cases:
- Advice itself is NOT a requirement. Only constraints ON the advice. - "Try to /
ideally / could / maybe" → not binding → not a requirement → check if substantive
enough for open_slot (see above); if not even substantive, drop entirely with no op.

"outcome 4", "fields": { "text": "<requirement text>", "type":
"<constraint|preference|ranking|task|other>" }, "creation action ids":
["<action id>"], "contributing action ids": [], "implementation action ids":
[], "related to": [], "explicit or implicit": "<explicit|implicit>", "rationale":
"<why extracted>" } ],
"open_slot ops": [ { "op": "open_slot", "slot id": "slot 1", "bound outcome id":

======================== TASK ========================
For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

UNDERSPECIFIED INTENT / PREFERENCE
Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement
Context-Grounded-Requirement | prior context, code, data, or situational
information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements

**contribution role**: SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER. Each listed action already
shows a role. If your judgment disagrees with the role shown, note it in
explanation rather than silently changing it.

Default to NO CONNECTION unless clear semantic evidence.

======================== OUTPUT FORMAT ========================
Return JSON only:
{ "slot origin labels": [ { "index": 0, "action id": "<e.g. A(1,34)>",

======================== TASK ========================
For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

UNDERSPECIFIED INTENT / PREFERENCE
Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement
Context-Grounded-Requirement | prior context, code, data, or situational
information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements

**contribution role**: SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER. Each listed action already
shows a role. If your judgment disagrees with the role shown, note it in
explanation rather than silently changing it.

Default to NO CONNECTION unless clear semantic evidence.

to find a paper about HCI", "User asks to find a paper in NLP conferences".
- Include evidence quote from the utterance.

**Role:** **SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER**
* **SHAPER:** Creates/shapes/revises the goal/requirement by proposing new ideas,
new tasks, constraints, alternatives, or directions.
* **EXECUTOR:** Executes/achieves the desired outcome/goal/requirement (e.g.,
drafting text, providing requested information, coding, searching, implementing).
* **OTHER:** Neither SHAPER nor EXECUTOR.

**Turn ID format:** each turn is labeled **U(x,y)** or **A(x,y)**, where **U**=user
turn, **A**=AI turn, **x**=the pair number this turn belongs to (the 1st user+AI
exchange is pair 1, the 2nd is pair 2, etc.), and **y**=the action's position
within that turn (1, 2, 3… in the order it was extracted). e.g. the user's 2nd
atomic action in pair 1 is "U(1,2)"; the AI's 1st atomic action in pair 3 is
"A(3,1)". Use this format for every turn id below — do not use plain integers.

Done

The mode you're describing — where a single creature's life is told as an epic, almost mythic, rather than clinically observed — has a specific home in his work. Search these:

The series to study: "Life Story" (BBC, 2014). This is the one. The whole series is built on exactly your device — it treats an animal's life as a hero's journey, structured in chapters: First Steps → Growing Up → Home → Power → Parenthood. Every episode narrates a creature's life as a single rising arc with stakes, setbacks, and a turn. That's structurally identical to what you're doing with your own life: origin → discovery → captivity → migration → now. Watch the "First Steps" episode opening, and the barnacle goose chick sequence (the chicks that have to leap off a cliff on day one to survive — pure metaphor for a terrifying first leap into the world).

Specific metaphorical life-arc clips to search:

"Attenborough mayfly one day to live" — a creature whose entire existence is compressed into a single day: born, must find purpose, mate, die by nightfall. He narrates it as the whole meaning of a life in miniature. This is the most philosophical he gets — life as a brief, urgent window. Closest to a "manifesto about existence" tone.

"Attenborough salmon journey home to spawn" — the return. A creature driven across an entire ocean by instinct to come home, against everything, for one purpose. Migration + sacrifice + destiny. Directly echoes your Kerala→Germany migration beat, but reframed as return to purpose.

"Attenborough emperor penguin march" (Frozen Planet / Dynasties) — endurance as epic. The long walk through hardship toward a goal.

What makes these the "metaphoric mode" vs. the clinical one — listen for the shift:

He stops describing behaviour ("the iguana freezes to avoid detection") and starts describing meaning ("this is a journey that will define the rest of its life"). The verbs change from observational to existential.

He assigns the creature intent and stakes — not "it does X," but "everything depends on this moment."

He zooms out to the universal — one animal's struggle stands for all of life's struggle.

For your film, the sweet spot is probably a blend: open in the wry clinical register ("this specimen calls itself a creative technologist" — funny, observed), then let it tilt into the metaphoric mode at the migration beat, where it stops being a joke and becomes an actual journey. That tonal shift — comic observation rising into mythic arc — is what'll make the ending land instead of trail off.

Watch the mayfly and the Life Story opening first. Those two will give you the metaphoric cadence fastest. Then bring your draft back.

Step 1b: Outcome Extraction

You are given ALL actions extracted from a complete dialogue. Your task:
(1) Identify **outcomes** (purpose-linked desired deliverables)
(2) Assign every action to exactly one outcome
(3) Maintain the dialogue summary: if the collaboration's core purpose is
unchanged since the prior summary, return it unchanged; if new actions
materially extend or shift that purpose, revise the summary to reflect the
fuller picture. Keep it to 1-2 sentences regardless of which case applies.

Every collaboration has a **purpose**. **Outcomes are purpose-linked desired
deliverables** --- concrete outputs that participants are actually working toward.
- **Outcomes = purpose-linked desired deliverables:** Deliverables are not limited
to text or documents --- they can be a **decision**, **advice**, **plan**,
**clarification**, or any other concrete result. Do **not** include things only
mentioned without being adopted as a goal. - **Requested or agreed outputs:** Any
concrete output a participant **explicitly requests** or participants **agree to
produce** is a deliverable. - **Primary output:** The main thing the dialogue aims
to produce must appear as an outcome. Sub-deliverables are children. - **Phrasing:**
e.g. "decision for X", "advice for X", "draft for X", "plan for X". - **Hierarchy:**
**parent** = abstract/general, **child** = specific/concrete. Use parent outcome id
/ child outcome ids. - **Granularity:** Prefer consolidated, task-level outcomes.
Example: "Workshop plan (date, venue, agenda)" NOT separate "Decide date", "Decide
time", etc. - **No duplicates.** Each distinct deliverable exactly once.

**REQUIREMENT** = An atomic, externally verifiable SUCCESS CONDITION for an outcome.
- Binary testable (pass/fail). - Must be explicitly stated or adopted as binding in
the dialogue.

**NOT a requirement:** content itself, advice, implementation methods, internal
reasoning, example outputs, one-off decisions.

Extract ONLY if ALL pass:
1) NECESSITY --- framed as mandatory (must/need/required/cannot, numeric
constraints, explicit include/exclude)
2) GROUNDING --- directly stated, no inference
3) REPLACEABILITY --- cannot be swapped without violating success
4) BINARY TESTABILITY --- reviewer can judge pass/fail

If an action raises a substantive idea, constraint, or decision point but FAILS
gate (1) NECESSITY specifically because of hedge or uncertainty language — this
includes option-uncertainty ("maybe", "could", "might", "may", "would possibly",
"perhaps", "mix / pick later"), epistemic hedges ("I think", "it seems", "I
believe", "it appears" — used to qualify the CONTENT of the claim, not as a
conversational filler elsewhere in the sentence), or approximation language
("about", "some", "most", "somewhat", "probably", "likely") — OR because the
action commits to determining something later rather than specifying it now
("I'll recommend based on your answer", "I'll decide after", "I'll verify before
committing") — do NOT discard it. Use the open_slot operation (see below) instead
of create. This applies only to Necessity failures caused by hedging, uncertainty,
or deferred content; failures of gates 2-4, content that is not substantive at all
(small talk, pure acknowledgment, restating something already said), or content
where a hedge word appears only as filler alongside an otherwise clearly mandatory
statement (e.g. "I think this must include X" — still a requirement, the hedge
qualifies the speaker's tone, not the requirement's content), are still handled
normally (dropped or extracted as create, respectively).

Operations:
- **create**: New requirement. Check PRIOR REQUIREMENTS above for revise BEFORE
create. - **revise**: Modify existing requirement (contradicts/tightens/
relaxes/replaces). Use EXACT existing req id (from PRIOR REQUIREMENTS) in related
to (within this outcome). - **delete**: Explicitly cancels a previously binding
condition. - **open_slot**: A hedged, uncertain, or deferred-content idea that
fails Necessity but is substantive enough to track — neither a binding requirement
nor nothing. Check PRIOR OPEN SLOTS above for resolve BEFORE open_slot: if this
action firmly answers/resolves a prior open slot, emit op="resolve" instead,
turning that slot into a new create with related to pointing at the resolved slot
id. Assign origin: "user" if the hedge came from the user's action, "AI" if from
the AI's action. A slot that has appeared in PRIOR OPEN SLOTS for 3 or more
consecutive pairs without being resolved should be marked op="abandon" — it is no
longer carried forward.

Edge cases:
- Advice itself is NOT a requirement. Only constraints ON the advice. - "Try to /
ideally / could / maybe" → not binding → not a requirement → check if substantive
enough for open_slot (see above); if not even substantive, drop entirely with no op.

Return JSON only (bound outcome id must be "outcome 3"):
{ "requirement ops": [ { "op": "create", "req id": "req 1", "bound outcome id":
"outcome 3", "fields": { "text": "<requirement text>", "type":
"<constraint|preference|ranking|task|other>" }, "creation action ids":
["<action id>"], "contributing action ids": [], "implementation action ids":
[], "related to": [], "explicit or implicit": "<explicit|implicit>", "rationale":
"<why extracted>" } ],
"open_slot ops": [ { "op": "open_slot", "slot id": "slot 1", "bound outcome id":
"outcome 3", "fields": { "text": "<the hedged, uncertain, or deferred idea>",
"type": "<constraint|preference|ranking|task|other>" }, "origin": "<user|AI>",
"creation action ids": ["<action id>"], "contributing action ids": [],
"related to": [], "explicit or implicit": "<explicit = directly posed as an open
question/uncertainty; implicit = inferred ambiguity nobody named>", "rationale":
"<why substantive but failed Necessity>" } ] }
Use action ids (e.g. "U(3,1)" or "A(3,1)") in creation action ids / contributing
action ids / implementation action ids.

[14] A(6,9) | Explain | EXECUTOR | AI explains the linguistic and stylistic differences between the 'metaphoric mode' and the 'clinical mode'. | evidence: "What makes these the "metaphoric mode" vs. the clinical one — listen for the shift: He stops describing behaviour... and starts describing meaning"
[15] A(6,10) | Suggest | SHAPER | AI suggests a tonal blend for the user's film project. | evidence: "For your film, the sweet spot is probably a blend: open in the wry clinical register... then let it tilt into the metaphoric mode at the migration beat"
[16] A(6,11) | Instruct | SHAPER | AI instructs the user on the next steps for their project. | evidence: "Watch the mayfly and the Life Story opening first. Those two will give you the metaphoric cadence fastest. Then bring your draft back."
[17] A(6,12) | Provide | EXECUTOR | AI provides a list of sources for further research. | evidence: "Sources: Attenborough’s Life Journey ~ About | Nature | PBS..."

======================== TASK ========================
For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

UNDERSPECIFIED INTENT / PREFERENCE
Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement
Context-Grounded-Requirement | prior context, code, data, or situational
information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements

**contribution role**: SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER. Each listed action already
shows a role. If your judgment disagrees with the role shown, note it in
explanation rather than silently changing it.

Default to NO CONNECTION unless clear semantic evidence.

======================== TASK ========================
For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

UNDERSPECIFIED INTENT / PREFERENCE
Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement
Context-Grounded-Requirement | prior context, code, data, or situational
information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements

**contribution role**: SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER. Each listed action already
shows a role. If your judgment disagrees with the role shown, note it in
explanation rather than silently changing it.

Default to NO CONNECTION unless clear semantic evidence.

======================== OUTPUT FORMAT ========================
Return JSON only:
{ "slot origin labels": [ { "index": 0, "action id": "<e.g. A(1,34)>",
"relationship type": "IMPLICIT CONNECTION", "relationship score": 3,
"explanation": "...", "contribution role": "AI" } ],
"preceding labels": [ { "index": 0, "action id": "<e.g. 4-1>",
"relationship type": "DIRECT CONNECTION", "relationship score": 5, "explanation":
"...", "contribution role": "SHAPER" } ],

"relationship type": "IMPLEMENTS", "relationship score": 5, "explanation":
"...", "contribution role": "EXECUTOR" } ] }
Include "slot origin labels" only if a KNOWN SLOT ORIGIN section was given
above. Include one entry for EVERY index in every section present.
Provide ONLY the JSON object. No additional text.

======================== TASK ========================
For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

UNDERSPECIFIED INTENT / PREFERENCE
Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement
Context-Grounded-Requirement | prior context, code, data, or situational
information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements

**contribution role**: SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER. Each listed action already
shows a role. If your judgment disagrees with the role shown, note it in
explanation rather than silently changing it.

Default to NO CONNECTION unless clear semantic evidence.

======================== TASK ========================
For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

UNDERSPECIFIED INTENT / PREFERENCE
Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement
Context-Grounded-Requirement | prior context, code, data, or situational
information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements

**contribution role**: SHAPER | EXECUTOR | OTHER. Each listed action already

For EVERY entry in every section given above, label the relationship to the
requirement.

**relationship type:**
- **DIRECT CONNECTION**: Action explicitly operates on the requirement --- creates,
tightens, relaxes, replaces, deletes, requests, evaluates, or fulfills it. The
requirement is the OBJECT of the action. - **IMPLICIT CONNECTION**: Action
provides context that influences, motivates, or triggers the requirement. Not
directly about the requirement itself. - **IMPLEMENTS**: (Section B only) Action
directly executes, fulfills, or produces output satisfying this requirement. -
**REVISES**: (Section B only) Action modifies, tightens, relaxes, or replaces the
requirement after it was established. - **CONTRIBUTES**: Action provides partial
work, context, or progress toward this requirement. - **NO CONNECTION**: No
meaningful relationship.

**relationship score** (required for DIRECT/IMPLICIT/IMPLEMENTS/REVISES/CONTRIBUTES):
- 1--3 for IMPLICIT CONNECTION or CONTRIBUTES:
  - 3 = Necessary: without this action, the requirement would likely not be
    established in its current form.
  - 2 = Supportive: meaningfully supports the requirement's establishment, but it
    could still be established without it.
  - 1 = Weak: minor or background connection; the requirement would likely still
    be established in a similar form without it.
- 4--5 for DIRECT CONNECTION, IMPLEMENTS, or REVISES (4=explicit, 5=state
  mutation / full fulfillment)
- null for NO CONNECTION

**explanation**: write as "<subtype>: <one-sentence justification>". Use the
following taxonomy as guidance, not as a closed set. The explanation should
reflect the actual actions and content of the conversation rather than merely
repeat the descriptions below.

Goal-Concretization | a prior broad goal later leads to a more concrete requirement
Preference-Explication | a prior implicit preference is later expressed as an
explicit requirement
ARTIFACT-TRIGGERED ELABORATION
Artifact-Triggered-Refinement | a prior artifact later prompts a refinement or
missing requirement

information later grounds a new requirement
Plan-Driven-Proceduralization | a prior plan or workflow later leads to a
procedural or next-step requirement
PROBLEM-TRIGGERED REVISION
Problem-Triggered-Correction | a prior failure, mismatch, or reported issue later
leads to a corrective requirement
Complexity-Triggered-Simplification | prior complexity, burden, or ambiguity later
leads to a simpler or narrower requirement
INTERACTIONAL STEERING
Option-Selection | prior alternatives are later resolved by selecting one option
Recommendation-Driven-Strategy | a prior request for guidance later leads to a
strategy, priority, or decision requirement
Extension-Driven-Next-Step | a prior invitation to continue later leads to a
concrete next-step requirement
Implementation-Triggered-Setup | a prior implementation request later leads to
added setup, execution, or usage requirements
TEMPORAL AND DIAGNOSTIC DYNAMICS
Incremental-Accumulation | prior preferences, constraints, or decisions accumulate
across turns and later shape the requirement
Hypothesis-And-Diagnosis | a prior causal hypothesis later leads to validation,
testing, or diagnostic requirements
